From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I came in because things at my apartment have gotten to the point where I can’t just ignore it anymore. The people there, they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and I know they’re keeping track of it. It’s not just me being sensitive or anything like that. I’ll go out, even just to the store, and when I come back there’ll be these papers or notes around, and everybody keeps saying, “Oh, those are just notices on the doors,” like regular apartment notices, but I don’t believe that’s all it is.\n\nIt feels like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like reporting on me, and then trying to make it look normal. I know how it sounds, but when you live there and you see the little things happening over and over, it’s different. I can tell it’s connected to me. So I figured I needed to come talk to somebody before it gets worse or before they use it against me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I’ve heard noises in the building, sure, like people moving around, doors closing, footsteps, that kind of thing. Sometimes it’s hard to tell where it’s coming from in an apartment place because sound carries funny through the walls and vents. But if you mean like voices talking to me when nobody’s there, I’m not sure I’d say that.\n\nI haven’t really sat there and thought, “That’s a voice nobody else can hear,” you know? It’s more that I hear things and then I’m trying to figure out if it’s from next door or outside or upstairs. Sometimes I’ll stop and listen because I want to know what’s going on, but I can’t say for sure it’s something only I hear.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that does happen, but it’s hard for me to tell while I’m doing it. In my head it connects, because I know the pieces, but when I’m trying to explain it out loud, people act like I jumped over the middle part.\n\nLike I’ll start talking about the rent, because that’s where one piece of it starts, and then I’ll mention the marina because that’s connected too, and then somehow I’m explaining about the choir robes and the wires in the walls, and people look at me like I just changed the subject three times. But to me it’s not changing the subject. It’s all part of the same thing, I just… I guess I don’t always lay it out in the order they expect.\n\nSometimes I can see it on their faces, like they’re waiting for me to come back around or they think I skipped half the story. And then I get frustrated because I’m trying to make it clear, but the more I try to explain, the more details come in, and then it probably sounds even more tangled. So yes, I guess I do have trouble expressing it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your thoughts are racing or difficult to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if I’d call it racing exactly, like fast thoughts, but it does get hard to control once I start trying to explain something. It’s like one thing pulls another thing behind it, and then another, and I’m already three rooms over in my head while the person listening is still back at the first part.\n\nLike I’ll start with the rent, because that’s where it begins for me, or at least that’s the part I can point to. Then the marina comes in because that connects, and then the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls, and I can see people’s faces change like I skipped half the story. But I didn’t skip it on purpose. In my mind those pieces are all tied together, it’s just hard to get them out in the right order.\n\nAnd then I try to back up and explain the part I missed, but that adds more pieces, and then I can feel it getting away from me. So yes, I guess they can be difficult to control, especially when I’m trying to make someone understand.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're having trouble sticking to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people tell me that, or they don’t exactly tell me, but I can see it. I’ll be talking and I think I’m staying on the same thing, because in my mind it’s all connected, but to them I guess it sounds like I’m wandering off.\n\nLike I said, I might start with the rent, because that’s the part that seems easiest to explain first. Then I bring up the marina, because that matters to it, and then the choir robes come into it, and the wires in the walls, and by then the person is looking at me like, “What are you talking about?” Like I skipped half the story. But I didn’t mean to skip it. I know the middle part in my head, I just don’t always say it out loud in the right order.\n\nSo yes, I do have trouble sticking to one topic, I guess. Or it looks that way. To me it still feels like one topic, just with a lot of pieces.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people watching you and the notes being left are all part of a larger plan or scheme?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t know every piece of it, and I know when I say it out loud people act like I’m making it bigger than it is, but that’s why I came in. Because at my apartment, people are watching when I leave and when I come back. It’s not just like neighbors noticing things normal, it feels like they’re keeping track.\n\nAnd then there are these notes being slipped around, like to the office, about me. Everybody keeps telling me, “Those are just notices on the doors,” like regular apartment notices, but that’s not how it feels from where I’m standing. The timing of them, and the way people look, and then something shows up on the door or near the office… it feels connected.\n\nSo yes, I do think there’s some kind of larger thing behind it. Maybe I don’t know who’s organizing it or what the whole plan is, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re watching my comings and goings and writing things down or passing notes, and then pretending it’s just normal apartment business.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of the people around you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I understand why you’re asking that. I do. People keep saying that to me in different ways, like maybe I’m reading too much into it, maybe it’s just regular notices on the doors, normal apartment stuff, and maybe people are just coming and going like anybody would.\n\nBut from where I’m standing, it doesn’t feel like that. That’s the part I keep trying to explain and I don’t think it comes out right. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. It’s the timing, the way they’re there, the way they look, the way things happen after. And then these notes show up or get slipped around, like to the office, and everybody says, “No, those are just notices,” but it feels like they’re about me.\n\nSo… could I be misinterpreting some small piece of it? Maybe, I guess. I’m not saying I know every single detail. But I don’t think the whole thing is just in my head or just me misunderstanding normal apartment notices. It feels too connected for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people watching you and the notes being left are connected to something specific that's happening in your life or something you've done?\n\nPatient: I think it’s connected to me, yes. I don’t know if it’s because of one exact thing I did, like there was some single moment where it started, but it feels like it’s about my comings and goings, like they’re keeping track of when I leave my apartment and when I come back.\n\nThat’s why I came in, because it’s not just a vague feeling. I’ll go out, or I’ll come back, and there are people around watching, or it seems like they’re waiting to see. Then later there’ll be something on the door, or something passed to the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Those are just notices on the doors, that’s normal,” but it doesn’t feel normal to me. It feels like the notes are about me, or because of me, even if they’re trying to make it look like regular apartment business.\n\nSo… specific? I guess the specific thing is me being there, my movements, what I’m doing day to day. I can’t tell you the whole reason they’re doing it, but it feels directed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that there could be another explanation for the things that are happening at your apartment, other than the one you've been thinking?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess there could be another explanation in the way people keep telling me there is. Like, they say it’s just notices on the doors, regular apartment things, and maybe people are just outside or in the hallway because they live there too. I understand that’s what it’s supposed to look like.\n\nBut that’s not why I came in. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It’s not one little thing by itself. It’s when I leave, when I come back, who’s around, the way it feels like they notice it, and then these notes show up or get slipped to the office. Everybody keeps saying, “No, that’s just a notice,” but it feels like it’s about me, like they’re keeping track and then passing it along.\n\nSo… could there be another explanation? Maybe for one part of it, maybe for a piece here or there. But when I put it all together, it doesn’t feel like regular apartment business to me. It feels directed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people watching you and the notes being left are part of a delusional system that you believe in strongly and can't be changed by reasoning?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I would call it that, what you just said. That sounds like… like you’re saying it’s some fixed thing in my head that can’t be reasoned with. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. I’ve listened when people tell me, “Those are just notices on the doors,” or “The office gets papers all the time,” or “People in apartments are going to be in the hallways.” I hear that.\n\nBut I came in because I know what’s been happening at my apartment. People are watching when I leave and when I come back, and it’s not just once or twice. It’s the pattern of it. Then these notes show up, or get slipped around to the office, and everybody keeps saying it’s just normal notices, but it feels like they’re about me. Like they’re keeping track of me and passing it along.\n\nSo if you’re asking, can somebody just talk me out of it by saying, “No, that’s not happening,” then… no, not really. Because I’ve already been told that, and it doesn’t fit with what I’m seeing. I’m not saying I understand every single part of it, but I don’t think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence or information that could change your mind about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve thought about that, because I know you’re asking if there’s something that would make me say, “Okay, I was wrong, it’s just normal apartment stuff.” And maybe if I could see everything, like if someone showed me exactly what those notes are and why they’re going to the office, and it really wasn’t about me… maybe that would help some.\n\nBut the hard part is, everybody already keeps telling me that. They say, “It’s just notices on the doors,” or “That’s just regular paperwork,” or “People are just around because they live there.” And I hear that, I really do. It’s not like I don’t understand what they’re saying. It’s just that it doesn’t match what I’m seeing and feeling there day after day.\n\nBecause I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. When I leave, when I come back, who’s around, how they seem to be paying attention, and then the notes showing up or getting slipped to the office… it all goes together to me. So if somebody just says, “No, that’s not happening,” that’s not enough to change my mind.\n\nI guess real proof would have to be more than just somebody explaining it away. I’d have to somehow know for sure the notes weren’t about me and that nobody was keeping track of me. But I don’t know how you’d prove that, because from where I’m standing, it feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anything could ever convince you that the people watching you and the notes being left are not actually about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “ever” is a hard word. I don’t want to sit here and say nothing could ever convince me, because then it sounds like I’m not listening at all. I am listening. I hear people when they tell me it’s just notices on the doors, or just regular apartment stuff, or the office gets papers and that doesn’t mean they’re about me.\n\nBut that’s the thing—everybody keeps saying that, and I still came in because I know what’s been happening. The people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It’s not just one person standing there one time. It’s the way it keeps lining up—when I leave, when I come back, who’s around, how it feels like they notice me, and then the notes or papers getting slipped around to the office. People can call them notices, but to me it feels like they’re connected to me.\n\nSo could something convince me? Maybe if I could see it all laid out in a way that made sense, like exactly what the notes were and why they weren’t about me. But just being told, “No, that’s not happening,” doesn’t change it. I’ve already heard that a lot. It would have to be something more than that, because from where I’m standing, it really feels like they’re keeping track of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing your train of thought while speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t always notice it right away, but I can tell by how people look at me. Like I’ll be trying to explain something, and in my head it all connects, but when it comes out, I’ll start with one thing—like the rent—and then I’m talking about the marina, and then somehow I’m on the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls, and people are just staring at me like I skipped over half the story.\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to confuse anybody. I feel like there are steps in between, but sometimes I don’t say all the steps, or I say them in the wrong order. Then I get frustrated because I can tell they’re not following me, and I have to back up and try again. Sometimes I’ll say, “No, wait, that’s not where I meant to start,” but by then I’m already in the middle of another part. So yes, I guess I do lose the thread sometimes when I’m talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could make you question or doubt your beliefs about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe something could make me pause, but it would have to be something real clear, not just people saying, “No, that’s not about you.” Because that’s what everybody keeps telling me already, that it’s just notices on the doors or regular papers going to the office, and I’m trying to hear that, I really am.\n\nBut I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. It’s the way they’re there, the way things happen after, the notes getting slipped around or ending up at the office. To me it all fits together. So if someone could actually show me what those notes are and prove they have nothing to do with me, maybe I’d question it a little. But just being told it’s normal apartment stuff doesn’t really reach me, because I’m the one living there and seeing how it keeps happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to keep your thoughts in order when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It’s like I know what I’m trying to say when it’s still in my head, but once I start talking, it doesn’t come out in the same order. I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like the rent, and then it reminds me of the marina, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then somehow I’m on the wires in the walls. To me there’s a connection, but I guess I don’t always say the parts in between.\n\nAnd then I can see it on people’s faces, like they’re trying to follow me but they can’t, like I skipped half the story. That makes me more nervous, and then I try to back up and explain it better, but sometimes I just get further into it. So yes, it’s hard to keep my thoughts in order when I’m trying to explain something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could ever make you completely change your mind about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: Completely change my mind? I don’t know… that’s hard, because I came in here because I know what’s been happening at my apartment. It’s not just one little thing I misunderstood. It’s the way people are around the building, like they notice when I leave and when I come back, and then these notes get slipped around or end up with the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Oh, that’s just notices on doors,” or “That’s normal apartment stuff.”\n\nBut to me it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like it’s about me. It feels connected. So for me to completely change my mind, I guess I’d have to be shown something very plain, like every note, who wrote it, why it was there, and that none of it had anything to do with me. Not just someone telling me to relax or that I’m reading into things, because people already say that, and it doesn’t change what I’m seeing.\n\nI’m not trying to be stubborn. I know you’re asking if there’s a chance I could be wrong, and I can say maybe, in my head, maybe there could be. But in my gut, when I’m there and I see them watching my comings and goings, it feels real. So completely change my mind? I don’t think words would do it. It would have to be something I could actually see and check for myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But just being told it’s normal apartment stuff doesn’t really reach me, because I’m the one living there and seeing how it keeps happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to keep your thoughts in order when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It’s like I know what I’m trying to say when it’s still in my head, but once I start talking, it doesn’t come out in the same order. I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like the rent, and then it reminds me of the marina, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then somehow I’m on the wires in the walls. To me there’s a connection, but I guess I don’t always say the parts in between.\n\nAnd then I can see it on people’s faces, like they’re trying to follow me but they can’t, like I skipped half the story. That makes me more nervous, and then I try to back up and explain it better, but sometimes I just get further into it. So yes, it’s hard to keep my thoughts in order when I’m trying to explain something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could ever make you completely change your mind about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: Completely change my mind? Yes… yes, I do. I don’t always notice it right at first, because in my head it feels like it makes sense, like I know why I’m bringing up one thing after another. But when I try to say it out loud, it can get all tangled.

Like I’ll start trying to explain something about the rent, and then that makes me think of the marina, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then somehow I’m onto the wires in the walls. And to me there’s a path there, there’s a reason I got from one thing to the next, but I guess I don’t always say all the middle parts. So people look at me like I skipped half the story, like they’re waiting for me to come back to the point.

That makes me feel embarrassed sometimes, or frustrated, because I’m not trying to be confusing. I’m trying to explain it right, but then I add more because I think the extra part will help, and sometimes it just makes it harder for people to follow. So, yeah, I do have a hard time putting it in a logical order when I’m talking.

In my head it connects, because I know the pieces, but when I’m trying to explain it out loud, people act like I jumped over the middle part.\n\nLike I’ll start talking about the rent, because that’s where one piece of it starts, and then I’ll mention the marina because that’s connected too, and then somehow I’m explaining about the choir robes and the wires in the walls, and people look at me like I just changed the subject three times. But to me it’s not changing the subject. It’s all part of the same thing, I just… I guess I don’t always lay it out in the order they expect.\n\nSometimes I can see it on their faces, like they’re waiting for me to come back around or they think I skipped half the story. And then I get frustrated because I’m trying to make it clear, but the more I try to explain, the more details come in, and then it probably sounds even more tangled. So yes, I guess I do have trouble expressing it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your thoughts are racing or difficult to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if I’d call it racing exactly, like fast thoughts, but it does get hard to control once I start trying to explain something. It’s like one thing pulls another thing behind it, and then another, and I’m already three rooms over in my head while the person listening is still back at the first part.\n\nLike I’ll start with the rent, because that’s where it begins for me, or at least that’s the part I can point to. Then the marina comes in because that connects, and then the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls, and I can see people’s faces change like I skipped half the story. But I didn’t skip it on purpose. In my mind those pieces are all tied together, it’s just hard to get them out in the right order.\n\nAnd then I try to back up and explain the part I missed, but that adds more pieces, and then I can feel it getting away from me. So yes, I guess they can be difficult to control, especially when I’m trying to make someone understand.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're having trouble sticking to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people tell me that, or they don’t exactly tell me, but I can see it. I’ll be talking and I think I’m staying on the same thing, because in my mind it’s all connected, but to them I guess it sounds like I’m wandering off.\n\nLike I said, I might start with the rent, because that’s the part that seems easiest to explain first. Then I bring up the marina, because that matters to it, and then the choir robes come into it, and the wires in the walls, and by then the person is looking at me like, “What are you talking about?” Like I skipped half the story. But I didn’t mean to skip it. I know the middle part in my head, I just don’t always say it out loud in the right order.\n\nSo yes, I do have trouble sticking to one topic, I guess. Or it looks that way. To me it still feels like one topic, just with a lot of pieces.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the people watching you and the notes being left are all part of a larger plan or scheme?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t know every piece of it, and I know when I say it out loud people act like I’m making it bigger than it is, but that’s why I came in. Because at my apartment, people are watching when I leave and when I come back. It’s not just like neighbors noticing things normal, it feels like they’re keeping track.\n\nAnd then there are these notes being slipped around, like to the office, about me. Everybody keeps telling me, “Those are just notices on the doors,” like regular apartment notices, but that’s not how it feels from where I’m standing. The timing of them, and the way people look, and then something shows up on the door or near the office… it feels connected.\n\nSo yes, I do think there’s some kind of larger thing behind it. Maybe I don’t know who’s organizing it or what the whole plan is, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re watching my comings and goings and writing things down or passing notes, and then pretending it’s just normal apartment business.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of the people around you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I understand why you’re asking that. I do. People keep saying that to me in different ways, like maybe I’m reading too much into it, maybe it’s just regular notices on the doors, normal apartment stuff, and maybe people are just coming and going like anybody would.\n\nBut from where I’m standing, it doesn’t feel like that. That’s the part I keep trying to explain and I don’t think it comes out right. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. It’s the timing, the way they’re there, the way they look, the way things happen after. And then these notes show up or get slipped around, like to the office, and everybody says, “No, those are just notices,” but it feels like they’re about me.\n\nSo… could I be misinterpreting some small piece of it? Maybe, I guess. I’m not saying I know every single detail. But I don’t think the whole thing is just in my head or just me misunderstanding normal apartment notices. It feels too connected for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people watching you and the notes being left are connected to something specific that's happening in your life or something you've done?\n\nPatient: I think it’s connected to me, yes. I don’t know if it’s because of one exact thing I did, like there was some single moment where it started, but it feels like it’s about my comings and goings, like they’re keeping track of when I leave my apartment and when I come back.\n\nThat’s why I came in, because it’s not just a vague feeling. I’ll go out, or I’ll come back, and there are people around watching, or it seems like they’re waiting to see. Then later there’ll be something on the door, or something passed to the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Those are just notices on the doors, that’s normal,” but it doesn’t feel normal to me. It feels like the notes are about me, or because of me, even if they’re trying to make it look like regular apartment business.\n\nSo… specific? I guess the specific thing is me being there, my movements, what I’m doing day to day. I can’t tell you the whole reason they’re doing it, but it feels directed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that there could be another explanation for the things that are happening at your apartment, other than the one you've been thinking?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess there could be another explanation in the way people keep telling me there is. Like, they say it’s just notices on the doors, regular apartment things, and maybe people are just outside or in the hallway because they live there too. I understand that’s what it’s supposed to look like.\n\nBut that’s not why I came in. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It’s not one little thing by itself. It’s when I leave, when I come back, who’s around, the way it feels like they notice it, and then these notes show up or get slipped to the office. Everybody keeps saying, “No, that’s just a notice,” but it feels like it’s about me, like they’re keeping track and then passing it along.\n\nSo… could there be another explanation? Maybe for one part of it, maybe for a piece here or there. But when I put it all together, it doesn’t feel like regular apartment business to me. It feels directed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people watching you and the notes being left are part of a delusional system that you believe in strongly and can't be changed by reasoning?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I would call it that, what you just said. That sounds like… like you’re saying it’s some fixed thing in my head that can’t be reasoned with. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. I’ve listened when people tell me, “Those are just notices on the doors,” or “The office gets papers all the time,” or “People in apartments are going to be in the hallways.” I hear that.\n\nBut I came in because I know what’s been happening at my apartment. People are watching when I leave and when I come back, and it’s not just once or twice. It’s the pattern of it. Then these notes show up, or get slipped around to the office, and everybody keeps saying it’s just normal notices, but it feels like they’re about me. Like they’re keeping track of me and passing it along.\n\nSo if you’re asking, can somebody just talk me out of it by saying, “No, that’s not happening,” then… no, not really. Because I’ve already been told that, and it doesn’t fit with what I’m seeing. I’m not saying I understand every single part of it, but I don’t think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence or information that could change your mind about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve thought about that, because I know you’re asking if there’s something that would make me say, “Okay, I was wrong, it’s just normal apartment stuff.” And maybe if I could see everything, like if someone showed me exactly what those notes are and why they’re going to the office, and it really wasn’t about me… maybe that would help some.\n\nBut the hard part is, everybody already keeps telling me that. They say, “It’s just notices on the doors,” or “That’s just regular paperwork,” or “People are just around because they live there.” And I hear that, I really do. It’s not like I don’t understand what they’re saying. It’s just that it doesn’t match what I’m seeing and feeling there day after day.\n\nBecause I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. When I leave, when I come back, who’s around, how they seem to be paying attention, and then the notes showing up or getting slipped to the office… it all goes together to me. So if somebody just says, “No, that’s not happening,” that’s not enough to change my mind.\n\nI guess real proof would have to be more than just somebody explaining it away. I’d have to somehow know for sure the notes weren’t about me and that nobody was keeping track of me. But I don’t know how you’d prove that, because from where I’m standing, it feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anything could ever convince you that the people watching you and the notes being left are not actually about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “ever” is a hard word. I don’t want to sit here and say nothing could ever convince me, because then it sounds like I’m not listening at all. I am listening. I hear people when they tell me it’s just notices on the doors, or just regular apartment stuff, or the office gets papers and that doesn’t mean they’re about me.\n\nBut that’s the thing—everybody keeps saying that, and I still came in because I know what’s been happening. The people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It’s not just one person standing there one time. It’s the way it keeps lining up—when I leave, when I come back, who’s around, how it feels like they notice me, and then the notes or papers getting slipped around to the office. People can call them notices, but to me it feels like they’re connected to me.\n\nSo could something convince me? Maybe if I could see it all laid out in a way that made sense, like exactly what the notes were and why they weren’t about me. But just being told, “No, that’s not happening,” doesn’t change it. I’ve already heard that a lot. It would have to be something more than that, because from where I’m standing, it really feels like they’re keeping track of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing your train of thought while speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t always notice it right away, but I can tell by how people look at me. Like I’ll be trying to explain something, and in my head it all connects, but when it comes out, I’ll start with one thing—like the rent—and then I’m talking about the marina, and then somehow I’m on the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls, and people are just staring at me like I skipped over half the story.\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to confuse anybody. I feel like there are steps in between, but sometimes I don’t say all the steps, or I say them in the wrong order. Then I get frustrated because I can tell they’re not following me, and I have to back up and try again. Sometimes I’ll say, “No, wait, that’s not where I meant to start,” but by then I’m already in the middle of another part. So yes, I guess I do lose the thread sometimes when I’m talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could make you question or doubt your beliefs about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe something could make me pause, but it would have to be something real clear, not just people saying, “No, that’s not about you.” Because that’s what everybody keeps telling me already, that it’s just notices on the doors or regular papers going to the office, and I’m trying to hear that, I really am.\n\nBut I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. It’s the way they’re there, the way things happen after, the notes getting slipped around or ending up at the office. To me it all fits together. So if someone could actually show me what those notes are and prove they have nothing to do with me, maybe I’d question it a little. But just being told it’s normal apartment stuff doesn’t really reach me, because I’m the one living there and seeing how it keeps happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to keep your thoughts in order when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It’s like I know what I’m trying to say when it’s still in my head, but once I start talking, it doesn’t come out in the same order. I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like the rent, and then it reminds me of the marina, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then somehow I’m on the wires in the walls. To me there’s a connection, but I guess I don’t always say the parts in between.\n\nAnd then I can see it on people’s faces, like they’re trying to follow me but they can’t, like I skipped half the story. That makes me more nervous, and then I try to back up and explain it better, but sometimes I just get further into it. So yes, it’s hard to keep my thoughts in order when I’m trying to explain something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could ever make you completely change your mind about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: Completely change my mind? I don’t know… that’s hard, because I came in here because I know what’s been happening at my apartment. It’s not just one little thing I misunderstood. It’s the way people are around the building, like they notice when I leave and when I come back, and then these notes get slipped around or end up with the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Oh, that’s just notices on doors,” or “That’s normal apartment stuff.”\n\nBut to me it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like it’s about me. It feels connected. So for me to completely change my mind, I guess I’d have to be shown something very plain, like every note, who wrote it, why it was there, and that none of it had anything to do with me. Not just someone telling me to relax or that I’m reading into things, because people already say that, and it doesn’t change what I’m seeing.\n\nI’m not trying to be stubborn. I know you’re asking if there’s a chance I could be wrong, and I can say maybe, in my head, maybe there could be. But in my gut, when I’m there and I see them watching my comings and goings, it feels real. So completely change my mind? I don’t think words would do it. It would have to be something I could actually see and check for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a logical and coherent manner?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I don’t always notice it right at first, because in my head it feels like it makes sense, like I know why I’m bringing up one thing after another. But when I try to say it out loud, it can get all tangled.\n\nLike I’ll start trying to explain something about the rent, and then that makes me think of the marina, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then somehow I’m onto the wires in the walls. And to me there’s a path there, there’s a reason I got from one thing to the next, but I guess I don’t always say all the middle parts. So people look at me like I skipped half the story, like they’re waiting for me to come back to the point.\n\nThat makes me feel embarrassed sometimes, or frustrated, because I’m not trying to be confusing. I’m trying to explain it right, but then I add more because I think the extra part will help, and sometimes it just makes it harder for people to follow. So, yeah, I do have a hard time putting it in a logical order when I’m talking.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I do, especially if it’s something that feels important or like I need people to understand it right. I start out with the thing I mean to talk about, like the rent, and then while I’m explaining that, it makes me think of the marina, because there’s some connection in my head, and then I’m talking about choir robes, and then somehow I’m talking about the wires in the walls.

And I know that sounds like I’m just jumping around, but while I’m saying it, it feels like there’s a thread connecting all of it. I’m not trying to wander off the subject. I’m trying to explain the whole picture, I guess. But then I see people looking at me like I skipped half the story, like they’re waiting for me to come back to what I was originally saying.

So yes, it’s hard for me to stick to one topic. Sometimes the more important it is, the worse it gets, because I feel like I have to include every piece so it makes sense. But then it comes out in a way that doesn’t make sense to other people.

That’s the part I keep trying to explain and I don’t think it comes out right. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. Then these notes show up, or get slipped around to the office, and everybody keeps saying it’s just normal notices, but it feels like they’re about me. Like they’re keeping track of me and passing it along.\n\nSo if you’re asking, can somebody just talk me out of it by saying, “No, that’s not happening,” then… no, not really. Because I’ve already been told that, and it doesn’t fit with what I’m seeing. I’m not saying I understand every single part of it, but I don’t think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence or information that could change your mind about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve thought about that, because I know you’re asking if there’s something that would make me say, “Okay, I was wrong, it’s just normal apartment stuff.” And maybe if I could see everything, like if someone showed me exactly what those notes are and why they’re going to the office, and it really wasn’t about me… maybe that would help some.\n\nBut the hard part is, everybody already keeps telling me that. They say, “It’s just notices on the doors,” or “That’s just regular paperwork,” or “People are just around because they live there.” And I hear that, I really do. It’s not like I don’t understand what they’re saying. It’s just that it doesn’t match what I’m seeing and feeling there day after day.\n\nBecause I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. When I leave, when I come back, who’s around, how they seem to be paying attention, and then the notes showing up or getting slipped to the office… it all goes together to me. So if somebody just says, “No, that’s not happening,” that’s not enough to change my mind.\n\nI guess real proof would have to be more than just somebody explaining it away. I’d have to somehow know for sure the notes weren’t about me and that nobody was keeping track of me. But I don’t know how you’d prove that, because from where I’m standing, it feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anything could ever convince you that the people watching you and the notes being left are not actually about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “ever” is a hard word. I don’t want to sit here and say nothing could ever convince me, because then it sounds like I’m not listening at all. I am listening. I hear people when they tell me it’s just notices on the doors, or just regular apartment stuff, or the office gets papers and that doesn’t mean they’re about me.\n\nBut that’s the thing—everybody keeps saying that, and I still came in because I know what’s been happening. The people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It’s not just one person standing there one time. It’s the way it keeps lining up—when I leave, when I come back, who’s around, how it feels like they notice me, and then the notes or papers getting slipped around to the office. People can call them notices, but to me it feels like they’re connected to me.\n\nSo could something convince me? Maybe if I could see it all laid out in a way that made sense, like exactly what the notes were and why they weren’t about me. But just being told, “No, that’s not happening,” doesn’t change it. I’ve already heard that a lot. It would have to be something more than that, because from where I’m standing, it really feels like they’re keeping track of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing your train of thought while speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t always notice it right away, but I can tell by how people look at me. Like I’ll be trying to explain something, and in my head it all connects, but when it comes out, I’ll start with one thing—like the rent—and then I’m talking about the marina, and then somehow I’m on the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls, and people are just staring at me like I skipped over half the story.\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to confuse anybody. I feel like there are steps in between, but sometimes I don’t say all the steps, or I say them in the wrong order. Then I get frustrated because I can tell they’re not following me, and I have to back up and try again. Sometimes I’ll say, “No, wait, that’s not where I meant to start,” but by then I’m already in the middle of another part. So yes, I guess I do lose the thread sometimes when I’m talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could make you question or doubt your beliefs about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe something could make me pause, but it would have to be something real clear, not just people saying, “No, that’s not about you.” Because that’s what everybody keeps telling me already, that it’s just notices on the doors or regular papers going to the office, and I’m trying to hear that, I really am.\n\nBut I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. It’s the way they’re there, the way things happen after, the notes getting slipped around or ending up at the office. To me it all fits together. So if someone could actually show me what those notes are and prove they have nothing to do with me, maybe I’d question it a little. But just being told it’s normal apartment stuff doesn’t really reach me, because I’m the one living there and seeing how it keeps happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to keep your thoughts in order when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It’s like I know what I’m trying to say when it’s still in my head, but once I start talking, it doesn’t come out in the same order. I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like the rent, and then it reminds me of the marina, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then somehow I’m on the wires in the walls. To me there’s a connection, but I guess I don’t always say the parts in between.\n\nAnd then I can see it on people’s faces, like they’re trying to follow me but they can’t, like I skipped half the story. That makes me more nervous, and then I try to back up and explain it better, but sometimes I just get further into it. So yes, it’s hard to keep my thoughts in order when I’m trying to explain something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could ever make you completely change your mind about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: Completely change my mind? I don’t know… that’s hard, because I came in here because I know what’s been happening at my apartment. It’s not just one little thing I misunderstood. It’s the way people are around the building, like they notice when I leave and when I come back, and then these notes get slipped around or end up with the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Oh, that’s just notices on doors,” or “That’s normal apartment stuff.”\n\nBut to me it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like it’s about me. It feels connected. So for me to completely change my mind, I guess I’d have to be shown something very plain, like every note, who wrote it, why it was there, and that none of it had anything to do with me. Not just someone telling me to relax or that I’m reading into things, because people already say that, and it doesn’t change what I’m seeing.\n\nI’m not trying to be stubborn. I know you’re asking if there’s a chance I could be wrong, and I can say maybe, in my head, maybe there could be. But in my gut, when I’m there and I see them watching my comings and goings, it feels real. So completely change my mind? I don’t think words would do it. It would have to be something I could actually see and check for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a logical and coherent manner?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I don’t always notice it right at first, because in my head it feels like it makes sense, like I know why I’m bringing up one thing after another. But when I try to say it out loud, it can get all tangled.\n\nLike I’ll start trying to explain something about the rent, and then that makes me think of the marina, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then somehow I’m onto the wires in the walls. And to me there’s a path there, there’s a reason I got from one thing to the next, but I guess I don’t always say all the middle parts. So people look at me like I skipped half the story, like they’re waiting for me to come back to the point.\n\nThat makes me feel embarrassed sometimes, or frustrated, because I’m not trying to be confusing. I’m trying to explain it right, but then I add more because I think the extra part will help, and sometimes it just makes it harder for people to follow. So, yeah, I do have a hard time putting it in a logical order when I’m talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to stick to one topic when speaking about something that's important to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do, especially if it’s something that feels important or like I need people to understand it right. I start out with the thing I mean to talk about, like the rent, and then while I’m explaining that, it makes me think of the marina, because there’s some connection in my head, and then I’m talking about choir robes, and then somehow I’m talking about the wires in the walls.\n\nAnd I know that sounds like I’m just jumping around, but while I’m saying it, it feels like there’s a thread connecting all of it. I’m not trying to wander off the subject. I’m trying to explain the whole picture, I guess. But then I see people looking at me like I skipped half the story, like they’re waiting for me to come back to what I was originally saying.\n\nSo yes, it’s hard for me to stick to one topic. Sometimes the more important it is, the worse it gets, because I feel like I have to include every piece so it makes sense. But then it comes out in a way that doesn’t make sense to other people.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Yes, I feel that a lot. I’ll be trying to explain something, and it makes sense in my head when I’m starting it, but then once I’m talking, it’s like I lose the straight line of it.

Like I’ll start with the rent, because that’s what I meant to talk about, and then the rent makes me think of the marina for some reason that feels connected to me, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls come into it. And I can see people’s faces change, like they’re trying to follow but they can’t, like I skipped half the story and didn’t tell them how I got from one part to the next.

It’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’m not trying to sound mixed up. I’m trying to explain it clearly. But then the more I try to explain, the more details I add, and sometimes that just makes it worse. So, yes, I do feel like my thoughts don’t make sense to other people when I say them out loud, even though inside my own head it feels like there’s a reason for all of it.

That sounds like… like you’re saying it’s some fixed thing in my head that can’t be reasoned with. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. I’ve listened when people tell me, “Those are just notices on the doors,” or “The office gets papers all the time,” or “People in apartments are going to be in the hallways.” I hear that.\n\nBut I came in because I know what’s been happening at my apartment. People are watching when I leave and when I come back, and it’s not just once or twice. It’s the pattern of it. Then these notes show up, or get slipped around to the office, and everybody keeps saying it’s just normal notices, but it feels like they’re about me. Like they’re keeping track of me and passing it along.\n\nSo if you’re asking, can somebody just talk me out of it by saying, “No, that’s not happening,” then… no, not really. Because I’ve already been told that, and it doesn’t fit with what I’m seeing. I’m not saying I understand every single part of it, but I don’t think it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence or information that could change your mind about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve thought about that, because I know you’re asking if there’s something that would make me say, “Okay, I was wrong, it’s just normal apartment stuff.” And maybe if I could see everything, like if someone showed me exactly what those notes are and why they’re going to the office, and it really wasn’t about me… maybe that would help some.\n\nBut the hard part is, everybody already keeps telling me that. They say, “It’s just notices on the doors,” or “That’s just regular paperwork,” or “People are just around because they live there.” And I hear that, I really do. It’s not like I don’t understand what they’re saying. It’s just that it doesn’t match what I’m seeing and feeling there day after day.\n\nBecause I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. When I leave, when I come back, who’s around, how they seem to be paying attention, and then the notes showing up or getting slipped to the office… it all goes together to me. So if somebody just says, “No, that’s not happening,” that’s not enough to change my mind.\n\nI guess real proof would have to be more than just somebody explaining it away. I’d have to somehow know for sure the notes weren’t about me and that nobody was keeping track of me. But I don’t know how you’d prove that, because from where I’m standing, it feels like it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anything could ever convince you that the people watching you and the notes being left are not actually about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… “ever” is a hard word. I don’t want to sit here and say nothing could ever convince me, because then it sounds like I’m not listening at all. I am listening. I hear people when they tell me it’s just notices on the doors, or just regular apartment stuff, or the office gets papers and that doesn’t mean they’re about me.\n\nBut that’s the thing—everybody keeps saying that, and I still came in because I know what’s been happening. The people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings. It’s not just one person standing there one time. It’s the way it keeps lining up—when I leave, when I come back, who’s around, how it feels like they notice me, and then the notes or papers getting slipped around to the office. People can call them notices, but to me it feels like they’re connected to me.\n\nSo could something convince me? Maybe if I could see it all laid out in a way that made sense, like exactly what the notes were and why they weren’t about me. But just being told, “No, that’s not happening,” doesn’t change it. I’ve already heard that a lot. It would have to be something more than that, because from where I’m standing, it really feels like they’re keeping track of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself losing your train of thought while speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t always notice it right away, but I can tell by how people look at me. Like I’ll be trying to explain something, and in my head it all connects, but when it comes out, I’ll start with one thing—like the rent—and then I’m talking about the marina, and then somehow I’m on the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls, and people are just staring at me like I skipped over half the story.\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to confuse anybody. I feel like there are steps in between, but sometimes I don’t say all the steps, or I say them in the wrong order. Then I get frustrated because I can tell they’re not following me, and I have to back up and try again. Sometimes I’ll say, “No, wait, that’s not where I meant to start,” but by then I’m already in the middle of another part. So yes, I guess I do lose the thread sometimes when I’m talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could make you question or doubt your beliefs about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe something could make me pause, but it would have to be something real clear, not just people saying, “No, that’s not about you.” Because that’s what everybody keeps telling me already, that it’s just notices on the doors or regular papers going to the office, and I’m trying to hear that, I really am.\n\nBut I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. It’s the way they’re there, the way things happen after, the notes getting slipped around or ending up at the office. To me it all fits together. So if someone could actually show me what those notes are and prove they have nothing to do with me, maybe I’d question it a little. But just being told it’s normal apartment stuff doesn’t really reach me, because I’m the one living there and seeing how it keeps happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to keep your thoughts in order when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It’s like I know what I’m trying to say when it’s still in my head, but once I start talking, it doesn’t come out in the same order. I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like the rent, and then it reminds me of the marina, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then somehow I’m on the wires in the walls. To me there’s a connection, but I guess I don’t always say the parts in between.\n\nAnd then I can see it on people’s faces, like they’re trying to follow me but they can’t, like I skipped half the story. That makes me more nervous, and then I try to back up and explain it better, but sometimes I just get further into it. So yes, it’s hard to keep my thoughts in order when I’m trying to explain something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could ever make you completely change your mind about the people watching you and the notes being left?\n\nPatient: Completely change my mind? I don’t know… that’s hard, because I came in here because I know what’s been happening at my apartment. It’s not just one little thing I misunderstood. It’s the way people are around the building, like they notice when I leave and when I come back, and then these notes get slipped around or end up with the office, and everybody keeps saying, “Oh, that’s just notices on doors,” or “That’s normal apartment stuff.”\n\nBut to me it doesn’t feel normal. It feels like it’s about me. It feels connected. So for me to completely change my mind, I guess I’d have to be shown something very plain, like every note, who wrote it, why it was there, and that none of it had anything to do with me. Not just someone telling me to relax or that I’m reading into things, because people already say that, and it doesn’t change what I’m seeing.\n\nI’m not trying to be stubborn. I know you’re asking if there’s a chance I could be wrong, and I can say maybe, in my head, maybe there could be. But in my gut, when I’m there and I see them watching my comings and goings, it feels real. So completely change my mind? I don’t think words would do it. It would have to be something I could actually see and check for myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to express your thoughts in a logical and coherent manner?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do. I don’t always notice it right at first, because in my head it feels like it makes sense, like I know why I’m bringing up one thing after another. But when I try to say it out loud, it can get all tangled.\n\nLike I’ll start trying to explain something about the rent, and then that makes me think of the marina, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then somehow I’m onto the wires in the walls. And to me there’s a path there, there’s a reason I got from one thing to the next, but I guess I don’t always say all the middle parts. So people look at me like I skipped half the story, like they’re waiting for me to come back to the point.\n\nThat makes me feel embarrassed sometimes, or frustrated, because I’m not trying to be confusing. I’m trying to explain it right, but then I add more because I think the extra part will help, and sometimes it just makes it harder for people to follow. So, yeah, I do have a hard time putting it in a logical order when I’m talking.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to stick to one topic when speaking about something that's important to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do, especially if it’s something that feels important or like I need people to understand it right. I start out with the thing I mean to talk about, like the rent, and then while I’m explaining that, it makes me think of the marina, because there’s some connection in my head, and then I’m talking about choir robes, and then somehow I’m talking about the wires in the walls.\n\nAnd I know that sounds like I’m just jumping around, but while I’m saying it, it feels like there’s a thread connecting all of it. I’m not trying to wander off the subject. I’m trying to explain the whole picture, I guess. But then I see people looking at me like I skipped half the story, like they’re waiting for me to come back to what I was originally saying.\n\nSo yes, it’s hard for me to stick to one topic. Sometimes the more important it is, the worse it gets, because I feel like I have to include every piece so it makes sense. But then it comes out in a way that doesn’t make sense to other people.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your thoughts are not making sense to others when you try to explain them?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel that a lot. I’ll be trying to explain something, and it makes sense in my head when I’m starting it, but then once I’m talking, it’s like I lose the straight line of it.\n\nLike I’ll start with the rent, because that’s what I meant to talk about, and then the rent makes me think of the marina for some reason that feels connected to me, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls come into it. And I can see people’s faces change, like they’re trying to follow but they can’t, like I skipped half the story and didn’t tell them how I got from one part to the next.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because I’m not trying to sound mixed up. I’m trying to explain it clearly. But then the more I try to explain, the more details I add, and sometimes that just makes it worse. So, yes, I do feel like my thoughts don’t make sense to other people when I say them out loud, even though inside my own head it feels like there’s a reason for all of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But when I try to say it out loud, it can get all tangled.\n\nLike I’ll start trying to explain something about the rent, and then that makes me think of the marina, and then I’m talking about the choir robes, and then somehow I’m onto the wires in the walls. I start out with the thing I mean to talk about, like the rent, and then while I’m explaining that, it makes me think of the marina, because there’s some connection in my head, and then I’m talking about choir robes, and then somehow I’m talking about the wires in the walls.\n\nAnd I know that sounds like I’m just jumping around, but while I’m saying it, it feels like there’s a thread connecting all of it. I’m not trying to wander off the subject. I’m trying to explain the whole picture, I guess. But then I see people looking at me like I skipped half the story, like they’re waiting for me to come back to what I was originally saying.\n\nSo yes, it’s hard for me to stick to one topic. Sometimes the more important it is, the worse it gets, because I feel like I have to include every piece so it makes sense. Yeah… I do. I know I’ve kind of been saying it already, but that’s exactly what happens to me. I’ll try to explain something simple, or something that should be simple, like the rent, and I think I’m starting in the right place. But then while I’m talking, my mind goes over to the marina, because in my head that connects somehow, and then I’m saying something about the choir robes, and then the wires in the walls come into it.

And I can hear myself doing it sometimes, but not always soon enough to stop it. It’s like I’m following a trail, but I’m the only one who can see the trail. Other people just hear me jumping from one thing to another. Then they look at me like I skipped half the story, like there was supposed to be a whole middle part that I didn’t say out loud.

That’s the part that bothers me, because I’m not trying to be unclear. I’m trying to make it easier to understand by adding what feels important, but then it comes out more confusing. So yes, I do have trouble expressing my thoughts in a way people can easily understand.

In my mind those pieces are all tied together, it’s just hard to get them out in the right order.\n\nAnd then I try to back up and explain the part I missed, but that adds more pieces, and then I can feel it getting away from me. So yes, I guess they can be difficult to control, especially when I’m trying to make someone understand.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're having trouble sticking to one topic when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people tell me that, or they don’t exactly tell me, but I can see it. Maybe I don’t know who’s organizing it or what the whole plan is, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re watching my comings and goings and writing things down or passing notes, and then pretending it’s just normal apartment business.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting the actions of the people around you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I understand why you’re asking that. I do. People keep saying that to me in different ways, like maybe I’m reading too much into it, maybe it’s just regular notices on the doors, normal apartment stuff, and maybe people are just coming and going like anybody would.\n\nBut from where I’m standing, it doesn’t feel like that. That’s the part I keep trying to explain and I don’t think it comes out right. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and when I come back. It’s the timing, the way they’re there, the way they look, the way things happen after. And then these notes show up or get slipped around, like to the office, and everybody says, “No, those are just notices,” but it feels like they’re about me.\n\nSo… could I be misinterpreting some small piece of it? It feels too connected for that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people watching you and the notes being left are connected to something specific that's happening in your life or something you've done?\n\nPatient: I think it’s connected to me, yes.
